=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a 2D integer matrix grid of size n x m, where each element is either 0, 1, or 2.
A V-shaped diagonal segment is defined as:

The segment starts with 1.
The subsequent elements follow this infinite sequence: 2, 0, 2, 0, ....
The segment:
	
Starts along a diagonal direction (top-left to bottom-right, bottom-right to top-left, top-right to bottom-left, or bottom-left to top-right).
Continues the sequence in the same diagonal direction.
Makes at most one clockwise 90-degree turn to another diagonal direction while maintaining the sequence.




Return the length of the longest V-shaped diagonal segment. If no valid segment exists, return 0.
 
Example 1:

Input: grid = [[2,2,1,2,2],[2,0,2,2,0],[2,0,1,1,0],[1,0,2,2,2],[2,0,0,2,2]]
Output: 5
Explanation:

The longest V-shaped diagonal segment has a length of 5 and follows these coordinates: (0,2) → (1,3) → (2,4), takes a 90-degree clockwise turn at (2,4), and continues as (3,3) → (4,2).

Example 2:

Input: grid = [[2,2,2,2,2],[2,0,2,2,0],[2,0,1,1,0],[1,0,2,2,2],[2,0,0,2,2]]
Output: 4
Explanation:

The longest V-shaped diagonal segment has a length of 4 and follows these coordinates: (2,3) → (3,2), takes a 90-degree clockwise turn at (3,2), and continues as (2,1) → (1,0).

Example 3:

Input: grid = [[1,2,2,2,2],[2,2,2,2,0],[2,0,0,0,0],[0,0,2,2,2],[2,0,0,2,0]]
Output: 5
Explanation:

The longest V-shaped diagonal segment has a length of 5 and follows these coordinates: (0,0) → (1,1) → (2,2) → (3,3) → (4,4).

Example 4:

Input: grid = [[1]]
Output: 1
Explanation:
The longest V-shaped diagonal segment has a length of 1 and follows these coordinates: (0,0).

 
Constraints:

n == grid.length
m == grid[i].length
1 <= n, m <= 500
grid[i][j] is either 0, 1 or 2.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def lenOfVDiagonal(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's maybe write a small pseudo-code to ensure no syntax errors.

```
from typing import List
from array import array

class Solution:
    def lenOfVDiagonal(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(grid)
        m = len(grid[0])
        total = n * m
        vals = [x for row in grid for x in row]
        dirs = [(1,1), (1,-1), (-1,-1), (-1,1)]
        cw = [1,2,3,0]
        run = [[array('H', [0]) * total for _ in range(2)] for _ in range(4)]
        for d in range(4):
            dr, dc = dirs[d]
            r0 = run[d][0]
            r2 = run[d][1]
            if dr == 1:
                rows = range(n-1, -1, -1)
            else:
                rows = range(n)
            for i in rows:
                base = i * m
                for j in range(m):
                    idx = base + j
                    val = vals[idx]
                    ni = i + dr
                    nj = j + dc
                    if 0 <= ni < n and 0 <= nj < m:
                        nidx = ni * m + nj
                        if val == 0:
                            r0[idx] = 1 + r2[nidx]
                        else:
                            r0[idx] = 0
                        if val == 2:
                            r2[idx] = 1 + r0[nidx]
                        else:
                            r2[idx] = 0
                    else:
                        r0[idx] = 1 if val == 0 else 0
                        r2[idx] = 1 if val == 2 else 0
        ans = 0
        for i in range(n):
            base = i*m
            for j in range(m):
                idx = base + j
                if vals[idx] == 1:
                    if ans < 1: ans = 1
                    for d in range(4):
                        dr, dc = dirs[d]
                        ni = i + dr
                        nj = j + dc
                        if 0 <= ni < n and 0 <= nj < m:
                            length = 1 + run[d][1][ni*m+nj]
                        else:
                            length = 1
                        if length > ans:
                            ans = length
        for d1 in range(4):
            dr, dc = dirs[d1]
            d2 = cw[d1]
            r0_d2 = run[d2][0]
            r2_d2 = run[d2][1]
            back0 = array('H', [0]) * total
            back1 = array('H', [0]) * total
            if dr == 1:
                rows = range(n)
            else:
                rows = range(n-1, -1, -1)
            for i in rows:
                base = i*m
                for j in range(m):
                    idx = base + j
                    val = vals[idx]
                    b0 = 0
                    b1 = 0
                    pi = i - dr
                    pj = j - dc
                    if 0 <= pi < n and 0 <= pj < m:
                        pidx = pi*m + pj
                        pval = vals[pidx]
                        if val == 0:
                            if pval == 2:
                                prev = back1[pidx]
                                if prev:
                                    b0 = prev + 1
                        elif val == 2:
                            if pval == 1:
                                b1 = 2
                            elif pval == 0:
                                prev = back0[pidx]
                                if prev:
                                    b1 = prev + 1
                    back0[idx] = b0
                    back1[idx] = b1
                    if b0:
                        cont = r0_d2[idx]
                        if cont:
                            cand = b0 + cont - 1
                            if cand > ans:
                                ans = cand
                    if b1:
                        cont = r2_d2[idx]
                        if cont:
                            cand = b1 + cont - 1
                            if cand > ans:
                                ans = cand
        return ans
```

Need check row order for back_len when dr==1: We process rows ascending. For d1=DR (dr=1), P=(i-1,j-1). For i=0, no P. For i=1, P row0 processed. Good. For d1=DL (dr=1), P=(i-1,j+1). For i=1, P row0 processed. Good. For dr==-1: rows descending. For d1=UL (dr=-1), P=(i+1,j+1). For i=n-1, no P. For i=n-2, P row n-1 processed. Good. For d1=UR (dr=-1), P=(i+1,j-1). For i=n-2, P row n-1 processed. Good.

Need check run arrays row order for dr==1: We process rows descending. For d=DR, neighbor (i+1,j+1). For i=n-1, no neighbor. For i=n-2, neighbor row n-1 processed. Good. For d=DL, neighbor (i+1,j-1). Good. For dr==-1: rows ascending. For d=UL, neighbor (i-1,j-1). For i=0, no neighbor. For i=1, neighbor row0 processed. Good. For d=UR, neighbor (i-1,j+1). Good.

But what about run0 at cell (i,j) uses r2[nidx] where nidx is in row i+1. r2 for that neighbor was computed when processing row i+1. Good. No dependency on j order.

Now, let's think about if in back_len computation, for d1=DL (dr=1,dc=-1), processing rows ascending but j ascending. P=(i-1,j+1). Row i-1 processed. Good. No dependency within row. Good.

Continues the sequence in the same diagonal direction. Makes at most one clockwise 90-degree turn to another diagonal direction while maintaining the sequence."

Clockwise turn increases bearing by 90° -> 135° = SE. In matrix coordinates, SE = down-right. Yes. So UR->DR. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer" if a
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List
from array import array

class Solution:
    def lenOfVDiagonal(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(grid)
        m = len(grid[0])
        total = n * m
        vals = [x for row in grid for x in row]

        # Directions: DR, DL, UL, UR
        dirs = [(1, 1), (1, -1), (-1, -1), (-1, 1)]
        cw = [1, 2, 3, 0]  # clockwise 90-degree turn

        # run[d][0] = run starting with 0, then 2,0,2,...
        # run[d][1] = run starting with 2, then 0,2,0,...
        run = [[array('H', [0]) * total for _ in range(2)] for _ in range(4)]

        for d in range(4):
            dr, dc = dirs[d]
            r0 = run[d][0]
            r2 = run[d][1]

            if dr == 1:
                rows = range(n - 1, -1, -1)
            else:
                rows = range(n)

            for i in rows:
                base = i * m
                for j in range(m):
                    idx = base + j
                    val = vals[idx]
                    ni = i + dr
                    nj = j + dc

                    if 0 <= ni < n and 0 <= nj < m:
                        nidx = ni * m + nj
                        if val == 0:
                            r0[idx] = 1 + r2[nidx]
                        else:
                            r0[idx] = 0

                        if val == 2:
                            r2[idx] = 1 + r0[nidx]
                        else:
                            r2[idx] = 0
                    else:
                        r0[idx] = 1 if val == 0 else 0
                        r2[idx] = 1 if val == 2 else 0

        ans = 0

        # Straight segments: start with 1, then 2,0,2,0,...
        for i in range(n):
            base = i * m
            for j in range(m):
                idx = base + j
                if vals[idx] == 1:
                    ans = max(ans, 1)
                    for d in range(4):
                        dr, dc = dirs[d]
                        ni = i + dr
                        nj = j + dc
                        if 0 <= ni < n and 0 <= nj < m:
                            ans = max(ans, 1 + run[d][1][ni * m + nj])
                        else:
                            ans = max(ans, 1)

        # Segments with one clockwise turn.
        for d1 in range(4):
            dr, dc = dirs[d1]
            d2 = cw[d1]
            r0_d2 = run[d2][0]
            r2_d2 = run[d2][1]

            back0 = array('H', [0]) * total  # phase 0: current value should be 0
            back1 = array('H', [0]) * total  # phase 1: current value should be 2

            if dr == 1:
                rows = range(n)
            else:
                rows = range(n - 1, -1, -1)

            for i in rows:
                base = i * m
                for j in range(m):
                    idx = base + j
                    val = vals[idx]
                    b0 = 0
                    b1 = 0

                    pi = i - dr
                    pj = j - dc

                    if 0 <= pi < n and 0 <= pj < m:
                        pidx = pi * m + pj
                        pval = vals[pidx]

                        if val == 0:
                            if pval == 2:
                                prev = back1[pidx]
                                if prev:
                                    b0 = prev + 1

                        elif val == 2:
                            if pval == 1:
                                b1 = 2
                            elif pval == 0:
                                prev = back0[pidx]
                                if prev:
                                    b1 = prev + 1

                    back0[idx] = b0
                    back1[idx] = b1

                    if b0:
                        cont = r0_d2[idx]
                        if cont:
                            ans = max(ans, b0 + cont - 1)

                    if b1:
                        cont = r2_d2[idx]
                        if cont:
                            ans = max(ans, b1 + cont - 1)

        return ans
```
